world.elements_dynamic[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n    }\n    \n    // Build connectivity: each element connects to its neighbors in 2D grid\n    for (int x = 0; x < n_elems_root; ++x) {\n        for (int y = 0; y < n_elems_root; ++y) {\n            const int idx = x * n_elems_root + y;\n            ElementStatic& elem = world.elements_static[idx];\n            \n            // Connect to neighbors (up, down, left, right)\n            const int offsets[4][2] = {{1, 0}, {-1, 0}, {0, 1}, {0, -1}};\n            \n            for (int n = 0; n < 4; ++n) {\n                const int nx = x + offsets[n][0];\n                const int ny = y + offsets[n][1];\n                \n                // Check if neighbor is within bounds\n                if (nx >= 0 && nx < n_elems_root && ny >= 0 && ny < n_elems_root) {\n                    const int neighbor_idx = nx * n_elems_root + ny;\n                    elem.connected_idx[elem.num_connections] = neighbor_idx;\n                    elem.connected_flux[elem.num_connections] = 1.0;\n                    elem.num_connections++;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Set corner elements as inflow/outflow to create interesting dynamics\n    const int last = n_elems_root - 1;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root + 0].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = OUTFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + 0].material_idx = OUTFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n}\n\n// Compute energy flux between two elements\ninline val_t computeFlux(const Material& mat, const ElementDynamic& this_elem,\n                        val_t connection_flux, const ElementDynamic& other_elem) {\n    return (other_elem.current_energy - this_elem.current_energy) * \n           mat.transfer_coeff * connection_flux * 0.25;\n}\n\n// Run simulation for n_iters iterations\nvoid runSimulation(World& world, const int n_iters) {\n    const size_t n_elems = world.elements_static.size();\n    \n    for (int iter = 0; iter < n_iters; ++iter) {\n        // Update all elements\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < n_elems; ++i) {\n            const ElementStatic& elem_static = world.elements_static[i];\n            const ElementDynamic& elem_dyn = world.elements_dynamic[i];\n            const Material& mat = world.materials[elem_static.material_idx];\n            \n            // Start with external flow\n            val_t total_flux = mat.external_flow;\n            \n            // Add flux from all connected elements\n            for (idx_t j = 0; j < elem_static.num_connections; ++j) {\n                const idx_t neighbor_idx = elem_static.connected_idx[j];\n                const ElementDynamic& neighbor_dyn = world.elements_dynamic[neighbor_idx];\n                total_flux += computeFlux(mat, elem_dyn, elem_static.connected_flux[j], neighbor_dyn);\n            }\n            \n            // Update element state\n            ElementDynamic& elem_write = world.elements_dynamic_swap[i];\n            elem_write.current_energy = elem_dyn.current_energy + total_flux;\n            elem_write.total_flux = elem_dyn.total_flux + std::abs(total_flux);\n        }\n        \n        // Swap buffers\n        std::swap(world.elements_dynamic, world.elements_dynamic_swap);\n    }\n}\n\n// Validate simulation results\nbool validateResults(const World& world) {\n    val_t energy_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t flux_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t energy_max = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::lowest();\n    val_t energy_min = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::max();\n    \n    for (const auto& elem : world.elements_dynamic) {\n        energy_sum += elem.current_energy;\n        flux_sum += elem.total_flux;\n        energy_max = std::max(elem.current_energy, energy_max);\n        energy_min = std::min(elem.current_energy, energy_min);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Validation results:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  Energy sum: %.12f\\n\", energy_sum);\n    printf(\"  Flux sum: %.2f\\n\", flux_sum);\n    printf(\"  Energy range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", energy_min, energy_max);\n    \n    // Check for numerical issues\n    constexpr val_t energy_epsilon = 1e-8;\n    \n    if (!std::isfinite(energy_sum)) {\n        printf(\"  ERROR: Energy sum is not finite\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    if (std::abs(energy_sum) > energy_epsilon) {\n        printf(\"  WARNING: Energy sum diverged from 0 (expected conservation)\\n\");\n        // Don't fail validation as this can happen with external flows\n    }\n    \n    if (!std::isfinite(flux_sum)) {\n        printf(\"  ERROR: Flux sum is not finite\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    if (!std::isfinite(energy_max) || !std::isfinite(energy_min)) {\n        printf(\"  ERROR: Energy extrema are not finite\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"  Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n    return true;\n}\n\n// Compute a simple hash of the results for verification\nuint64_t computeHash(const std::vector<ElementDynamic>& elements) {\n    uint64_t hash = 0;\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < elements.size(); ++i) {\n        // Simple hash combining energy and flux values\n        const uint64_t* e_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].current_energy);\n        const uint64_t* f_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].total_flux);\n        hash ^= (*e_ptr + i) * 0x9e3779b97f4a7c15ULL;\n        hash ^= (*f_ptr + i) * 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9ULL;\n    }\n    return hash;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Grid size (NxN elements) (default: 512)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -i <num>     Number of simulation iterations (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    int n_elems_root = 512;\n    int n_iters = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n    \n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            n_elems_root = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-i\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            n_iters = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    printf(\"Unstructured Mesh Energy Transfer Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"============================================\\n\");\n    printf(\"Grid size: %d x %d = %d elements\\n\", n_elems_root, n_elems_root, n_elems);\n    printf(\"Iterations: %d\\n\", n_iters);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    printf(\"\\n\");\n    \n    // Build the unstructured mesh\n    printf(\"Building unstructured mesh...\\n\");\n    World world;\n    buildSquare2D(world, n_elems_root);\n    \n    // Calculate memory usage\n    const size_t static_mem = world.elements_static.size() * sizeof(ElementStatic);\n    const size_t dynamic_mem = world.elements_dynamic.size() * sizeof(ElementDynamic) * 2;\n    const size_t total_mem = static_mem + dynamic_mem;\n    printf(\"Memory usage: %.2f MB (static: %.2f MB, dynamic: %.2f MB)\\n\",\n           total_mem / (1024.0 * 1024.0),\n           static_mem / (1024.0 * 1024.0),\n           dynamic_mem / (1024.0 * 1024.0));\n    printf(\"\\n\");\n    \n    // Run simulation\n    printf(\"Running simulation...\\n\");\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    \n    runSimulation(world, n_iters);\n    \n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration_ms = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start).count();\n    \n    printf(\"Computation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration_ms);\n    \n    // Calculate performance metrics\n    const int n_measured_iters = std::max(n_iters - 1, 1);\n    const double time_per_iter = static_cast<double>(duration_ms) / n_measured_iters;\n    const double giga_elems_per_sec = (n_measured_iters * n_elems) / (duration_ms / 1000.0) / 1e9;\n    \n    // Approximate FLOPS: ~22 FLOPS per element per iteration (from reference)\n    const double gflops = giga_elems_per_sec * 22.0;\n    \n    printf(\"Performance:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  Time per iteration: %.4f ms\\n\", time_per_iter);\n    printf(\"  Elements/sec: %.4f GigaElements/s\\n\", giga_elems_per_sec);\n    printf(\"  Performance: %.4f GFLOPS\\n\", gflops);\n    \n    // Compute hash for verification\n    const uint64_t hash = computeHash(world.elements_dynamic);\n    printf(\"  Result hash: %016lX\\n\", hash);\n    printf(\"\\n\");\n    \n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        std::vector<double> energyData;\n        energyData.reserve(world.elements_dynamic.size());\n        for (const auto& elem : world.elements_dynamic) {\n            energyData.push_back(elem.current_energy);\n        }\n        print_results(energyData, \"ElementEnergy\");\n    }\n    \n    // Validation\n    if (validate) {\n        bool valid = validateResults(world);\n        if (!valid) {\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    return \"../common/results_output.hpp\"\n\n// Types to represent unstructured mesh elements\nusing idx_t = uint64_t;\nusing val_t = double;\n\n// Maximum number of connections per element (for a 2D grid: 4 neighbors)\nconstexpr int MAX_CONNECTIONS = 8;\n\n// Material properties for energy transfer\nstruct Material {\n    val_t transfer_coeff;  // Energy transfer coefficient\n    val_t external_flow;   // External energy source/sink\n};\n\n// Static connectivity information for each element\nstruct ElementStatic {\n    idx_t material_idx;\n    idx_t num_connections;\n    idx_t connected_idx[MAX_CONNECTIONS];     // Indices of connected elements\n    val_t connected_flux[MAX_CONNECTIONS];    // Flux coefficients for each connection\n};\n\n// Dynamic state for each element\nstruct ElementDynamic {\n    val_t current_energy;\n    val_t total_flux;\n};\n\n// World state\nstruct World {\n    std::vector<Material> materials;\n    std::vector<ElementStatic> elements_static;\n    std::vector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic;\n    std::vector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic_swap;\n};\n\n// Material type IDs\nconstexpr idx_t DEFAULT_MAT_ID = 0;\nconstexpr idx_t INFLOW_MAT_ID = 1;\nconstexpr idx_t OUTFLOW_MAT_ID = 2;\n\n// Build a 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    // Initialize materials\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.0});    // Default material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.5});    // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize all elements with default material and zero energy\n    for (int i = 0; i < n_elems; ++i) {\n        world.elements_static[i].material_idx = DEFAULT_MAT_ID;\n        world.elements_static[i].num_connections = 0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n    }\n    \n    // Build connectivity: each element connects to its neighbors in 2D grid\n    for (int x = 0; x < n_elems_root; ++x) {\n        for (int y = 0; y < n_elems_root; ++y) {\n            const int idx = x * n_elems_root + y;\n            ElementStatic& elem = world.elements_static[idx];\n            \n            // Connect to neighbors (up, down, left, right)\n            const int offsets[4][2] = {{1, 0}, {-1, 0}, {0, 1}, {0, -1}};\n            \n            for (int n = 0; n < 4; ++n) {\n                const int nx = x + offsets[n][0];\n                const int ny = y + offsets[n][1];\n                \n                // Check if neighbor is within bounds\n                if (nx >= 0 && nx < n_elems_root && ny >= 0 && ny < n_elems_root) {\n                    const int neighbor_idx = nx * n_elems_root + ny;\n                    elem.connected_idx[elem.num_connections] = neighbor_idx;\n                    elem.connected_flux[elem.num_connections] = 1.0;\n                    elem.num_connections++;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Set corner elements as inflow/outflow to create interesting dynamics\n    const int last = n_elems_root - 1;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root +    world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n}\n\n// Compute energy flux between two elements\ninline val_t computeFlux(const Material& mat, const ElementDynamic& this_elem,\n                        val_t connection_flux, const ElementDynamic& other_elem) {\n    return (other_elem.current_energy - this_elem.current_energy) * \n           mat.transfer_coeff * connection_flux * 0.25;\n}\n\n// Run simulation for n_iters iterations\nvoid runSimulation(World& world, const int n_iters) {\n    const size_t n_elems = world.elements_static.size();\n    \n    for (int iter = 0; iter < n_iters; ++iter) {\n        // Update all elements\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < n_elems; ++i) {\n            const ElementStatic& elem_static = world.elements_static[i];\n            const ElementDynamic& elem_dyn = world.elements_dynamic[i];\n            const Material& mat = world.materials[elem_static.material_idx];\n            \n            // Start with external flow\n            val_t total_flux = mat.external_flow;\n            \n            // Add flux from all connected elements\n            for (idx_t j = 0; j < elem_static.num_connections; ++j) {\n                const idx_t neighbor_idx = elem_static.connected_idx[j];\n                const ElementDynamic& neighbor_dyn = world.elements_dynamic[neighbor_idx];\n                total_flux += computeFlux(mat, elem_dyn, elem_static.connected_flux[j], neighbor_dyn);\n            }\n            \n            // Update element state\n            ElementDynamic& elem_write = world.elements_dynamic_swap[i];\n            elem_write.current_energy = elem_dyn.current_energy + total_flux;\n            elem_write.total_flux = elem_dyn.total_flux + std::abs(total_flux);\n        }\n        \n        // Swap buffers\n        std::swap(world.elements_dynamic, world.elements_dynamic_swap);\n    }\n}\n\n// Validate simulation results\nbool validateResults(const World& world) {\n    val_t energy_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t flux_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t energy_max = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::lowest();\n    val_t energy_min = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::max();\n    \n    for (const auto& elem : world.elements_dynamic) {\n        energy_sum += elem.current_energy;\n        flux_sum += elem.total_flux;\n        energy_max = std::max(elem.current_energy, energy_max);\n        energy_min = std::min(elem.current_energy, energy_min);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Validation results:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  Energy sum: %.12f\\n\", energy_sum);\n    printf(\"  Flux sum: %.2f\\n\", flux_sum);\n    printf(\"  Energy range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", energy_min, energy_max);\n    \n    // Check for numerical issues\n    constexpr val_t energy_epsilon = 1e-8;\n    \n    if (!std::isfinite(energy_sum)) {\n        printf(\"  ERROR: Energy sum is not finite\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    if (std::abs(energy_sum) > energy_epsilon) {\n        printf(\"  WARNING: Energy sum diverged from 0 (expected conservation)\\n\");\n        // Don't fail validation as this can happen with external flows\n    }\n    \n    if (!std::isfinite(flux_sum)) {\n        printf(\"  ERROR: Flux sum is not finite\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    if (!std::isfinite(energy_max) || !std::isfinite(energy_min)) {\n        printf(\"  ERROR: Energy extrema are not finite\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"  Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n    return true;\n}\n\n// Compute a simple hash of the results for verification\nuint64_t computeHash(const std::vector<ElementDynamic>& elements) {\n    uint64_t hash = 0;\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < elements.size(); ++i) {\n        // Simple hash combining energy and flux values\n        const uint64_t* e_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].current_energy);\n        const uint64_t* f_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].total_flux);\n        hash ^= (*e_ptr + i) * 0x9e3779b97f4a7c15ULL;\n        hash ^= (*f_ptr + i) * 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9ULL;\n    }\n    return hash;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Grid size (NxN elements) (default: 512)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -i <num>     Number of simulation iterations (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    int n_elems_root = 512;\n    int n_iters = 10;\n This keeps std::vector::resize from touching every page from the\n// master thread, so that the *first touch* of each page happens inside the parallel\n// initialization loops. On a multi-socket machine this is what places the pages on the\n// NUMA node of the thread that will later work on them.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct NoInitAllocator {\n    using value_type = T;\n\n    NoInitAllocator() = default;\n    template <typename U>\n    constexpr NoInitAllocator(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) noexcept {}\n\n    T* allocate(std::size_t n) {\n        return static_cast<T*>(::operator new(n * sizeof(T)));\n    }\n    void deallocate(T* p, std::size_t) noexcept { ::operator delete(p); }\n\n    // Default-init: no zero filling for trivial types\n    template <typename U>\n    void construct(U* p) noexcept(std::is_nothrow_default_constructible_v<U>) {\n        ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U;\n    }\n    template <typename U, typename... nvector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic_swap;\n};\n\n// Material type IDs\nconstexpr idx_t DEFAULT_MAT_ID = 0;\nconstexpr idx_t INFLOW_MAT_ID = 1;\nconstexpr idx_t OUTFLOW_MAT_ID = 2;\n\n// Build a 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize all elements with default material and zero energy\n    for (int i = 0; i < n_elems; j < elem_static.num_connections; ++j) {\n                    const idx_t neighbor_idx = elem_static.connected_idx[j];\n                    const ElementDynamic& neighbor_dyn = read_buf[neighbor_idx];\n                    total_flux += computeFlux(mat, elem_dyn, elem_static.connected_flux[j], neighbor_dyn);\n                }\n\n                // Update element state\n                ElementDynamic& elem_write = write_buf[i];\n                elem_write.current_energy = elem_dyn.current_energy + total_flux;\n                elem_write.total_flux = elem_dyn.total_flux + std::abs(total_flux);\n            }\n            // (implicit barrier at the end of the worksharing loop)\n\n            // Swap buffers\n            std::swap(read_buf, write_buf);\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Reflect the buffer flips in the world state: an even number of swaps is a no-op.\n    if (n_iters % 2 != 0) {\n        std::swap(world.elements_dynamic, world.elements_dynamic_swap);\n    }\n}","originalFile":"#include <algorithm>\n#include <chrono>\n#include <cmath>\n#include <cstdint>\n#include <cstdio>\n#include <cstdlib>\n#include <cstring>\n#include <limits>\n#include <memory>\n#include <new>\n#include <utility>\n#include <vector>\n\n#include <omp.h>\n\n#include \"../common/results_output.hpp\"\n\n// Types to represent unstructured mesh elements\nusing idx_t = uint64_t;\nusing val_t = double;\n\n// Allocator that default-initializes (i.e. leaves POD memory untouched) instead of\n// value-initializing. This keeps std::vector::resize from touching every page from the\n// master thread, so that the *first touch* of each page happens inside the parallel\n// initialization loops. On a multi-socket machine this is what places the pages on the\n// NUMA node of the thread that will later work on them.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct NoInitAllocator {\n    using value_type = T;\n\n    NoInitAllocator() = default;\n    template <typename U>\n    constexpr NoInitAllocator(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) noexcept {}\n\n    T* allocate(std::size_t n) {\n        return static_cast<T*>(::operator new(n * sizeof(T)));\n    }\n    void deallocate(T* p, std::size_t) noexcept { ::operator delete(p); }\n\n    // Default-init: no zero filling for trivial types\n    template <typename U>\n    void construct(U* p) noexcept(std::is_nothrow_default_constructible_v<U>) {\n        ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U;\n    }\n    template <typename U, typename... Args>\n    void construct(U* p, Args&&... args) {\n        ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U(std::forward<Args>(args)...);\n    }\n\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator==(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return true; }\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator!=(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return false; }\n};\n\ntemplate <typename T>\nusing nvector = std::vector<T, NoInitAllocator<T>>;\n\n// Maximum number of connections per element (for a 2D grid: 4 neighbors)\nconstexpr int MAX_CONNECTIONS = 8;\n\n// Material properties for energy transfer\nstruct Material {\n    val_t transfer_coeff;  // Energy transfer coefficient\n    val_t external_flow;   // External energy source/sink\n};\n\n// Static connectivity information for each total_flux;\n};\n\n// World state\nstruct World {\n    std::vector<Material> materials;\n    nvector<ElementStatic> elements_static;\n    nvector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic;\n    nvector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic_swap;\n};\n\n// Material type IDs\nconstexpr idx_t DEFAULT_MAT_ID = 0;\nconstexpr idx_t INFLOW_MAT_ID = 1;\nconstexpr idx_t OUTFLOW_MAT_ID = 2;\n\n// Build a 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    // Initialize materials\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.0});    // Default material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.5});    // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize all elements with default material and zero energy.\n    // This loop uses the same iteration space and schedule as the simulation kernel, so\n    // it also performs the NUMA-local first touch of all element data.\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < static_cast<size_t>(n_elems); ++i) {\n        world.elements_static[i].material_idx = DEFAULT_MAT_ID;\n        world.elements_static[i].num_connections = 0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic_swap[i].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic_swap[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n    }\n\n    // Build connectivity: each element connects to its neighbors in 2D grid\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (int x = 0; x < n_elems_root; ++x) {\n        for (int y = 0; y < n_elems_root; ++y) {\n            const int idx = x * n_elems_root + y;\n            ElementStatic& elem = world.elements_static[idx];\n            \n            // Connect to neighbors (up, down, left, right)\n            const int offsets[4][2] = {{1, 0}, {-1, 0}, {0, 1}, {0, -1}};\n            \n            for (int n = 0; n < 4; ++n) {\n                const int nx = x + offsets[n][0];\n                const int ny = y + offsets[n][1];\n                \n                // Check if neighbor is within bounds\n                if (nx >= 0 && nx < n_elems_root && ny >= 0 && ny < n_elems_root) {\n                    const int neighbor_idx = nx * n_elems_root + ny;\n                    elem.connected_idx[elem.num_connections] = neighbor_idx;\n                    elem.connected_flux[elem.num_connections] = 1.0;\n                    elem.num_connections++;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Set      val_t connection_flux, const ElementDynamic& other_elem) {\n    return (other_elem.current_energy - this_elem.current_energy) * \n           mat.transfer_coeff * connection_flux * 0.25;\n}\n\n// This keeps std::vector::resize from touching every page from the\n// master thread, so that the *first touch* of each page happens inside the parallel\n// initialization loops. On a multi-socket machine this is what places the pages on the\n// NUMA node of the thread that will later work on them.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct NoInitAllocator {\n    using value_type = T;\n\n    NoInitAllocator() = default;\n    template <typename U>\n    constexpr NoInitAllocator(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) noexcept {}\n\n    T* allocate(std::size_t n) {\n        return static_cast<T*>(::operator new(n * sizeof(T)));\n    }\n    void deallocate(T* p, std::size_t) noexcept { ::operator delete(p); }\n\n    // Default-init: no zero filling for trivial types\n    template <typename U>\n    void construct(U* p) noexcept(std::is_nothrow_default_constructible_v<U>) {\n        ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U;\n    }\n    template <typename U, typename... NoInitAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return true; }\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator!=(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return false; }\n};\n\ntemplate <typename T>\nusing nvector = std::vector<T, NoInitAllocator<T>>;\n\n// Maximum number of connections per element (for a 2D grid: 4 neighbors)\nconstexpr int MAX_CONNECTIONS = 8;\n\n// Material properties for energy 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    // Initialize materials\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.0});    // Default material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.5});    // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize all elements with default material and zero energy.\n    // This loop uses the same iteration space and schedule as the simulation kernel, so\n    // it also performs the NUMA-local first touch of all element data.\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < static_cast<size_t>(n_elems); ++i) {\n        world.elements_static[i].material_idx = DEFAULT_MAT_ID;\n        world.elements_static[i].num_connections = 0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic_swap[i].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic_swap[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n    }\n\n    // Build connectivity: each element connects to its neighbors in 2D grid\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (int x =     \n            // Connect to neighbors (up, down, left, right)\n            const int offsets[4][2] = {{1, 0}, {-1, 0}, {0, 1}, {0, -1}};\n            \n            for (int n = 0; n < 4; ++n) {\n && nx < n_elems_root && ny >= 0 && ny < n_elems_root) {\n                    const int neighbor_idx = nx * n_elems_root + ny;\n                    elem.connected_idx[elem.num_connections] = neighbor_idx;\n                    elem.connected_flux[elem.num_connections] = 1.0;\n                    elem.num_connections++;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Set      const uint64_t* f_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(&elements[i].total_flux);\n        hash ^= (*e_ptr + i) * 0x9e3779b97f4a7c15ULL;\n        hash ^= (*f_ptr + i) * 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9ULL;\n This keeps std::vector::resize from touching every page from the\n// master thread, so that the *first touch* of each page happens inside the parallel\n// initialization loops. On a multi-socket machine this is what places the pages on the\n// NUMA node of the thread that will later work on them.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct NoInitAllocator {\n    using value_type = T;\n\n    NoInitAllocator() = default;\n    template <typename U>\n    constexpr NoInitAllocator(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) noexcept {}\n\n    T* allocate(std::size_t n) {\n        return static_cast<T*>(::operator new(n * sizeof(T)));\n    }\n    void deallocate(T* p, std::size_t) noexcept { ::operator delete(p); }\n\n    // Default-init: no zero filling for trivial types\n    template <typename U>\n    void construct(U* p) noexcept(std::is_nothrow_default_constructible_v<U>) {\n        ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U;\n    }\n    template <typename U, typename... Args>\n    void construct(U* p, Args&&... args) {\n        ::new (static_cast<void*>(p)) U(std::forward<Args>(args)...);\n    }\n\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator==(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return true; }\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator!=(const NoInitAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return false; }\n};\n\ntemplate <typename T>\nusing nvector = std::vector<T, NoInitAllocator<T>>;\n\n// Maximum number of connections per element (for a 2D grid: 4 neighbors)\nconstexpr int MAX_CONNECTIONS = 8;\n\n// Material properties for energy transfer\nstruct Material {\n    val_t transfer_coeff;  // Energy transfer coefficient\n    val_t external_flow;   // External energy source/sink\n};\n\n// Static connectivity information for each element\nstruct ElementStatic {\n    idx_t material_idx;\n    idx_t num_connections;\n    idx_t connected_idx[MAX_CONNECTIONS];     // Indices of connected elements\n    val_t connected_flux[MAX_CONNECTIONS];    // Flux coefficients for each connection\n};\n\n// Dynamic state for each element\nstruct ElementDynamic {\n    val_t current_energy;\n    val_t total_flux;\n};\n\n// World state\nstruct World {\n    std::vector<Material> materials;\n    nvector<ElementStatic> elements_static;\n    nvector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic;\n    nvector<ElementDynamic> elements_dynamic_swap;\n};\n\n// Material type IDs\nconstexpr idx_t DEFAULT_MAT_ID = 0;\nconstexpr idx_t INFLOW_MAT_ID = 1;\nconstexpr idx_t OUTFLOW_MAT_ID = 2;\n\n// Build a 2D square grid as an unstructured mesh\n// This represents computation on arbitrarily-shaped geometries\nvoid buildSquare2D(World& world, const int n_elems_root) {\n    const int n_elems = n_elems_root * n_elems_root;\n    \n    // Initialize materials\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.0});    // Default material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, 0.5});    // Inflow material\n    world.materials.emplace_back(Material{0.8, -0.5});   // Outflow material\n    \n    // Allocate elements\n    world.elements_static.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic.resize(n_elems);\n    world.elements_dynamic_swap.resize(n_elems);\n    \n    // Initialize all elements with default material and zero energy.\n    // This loop uses the same iteration space and schedule as the simulation kernel, so\n    // it also performs the NUMA-local first touch of all element data.\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < static_cast<size_t>(n_elems); ++i) {\n        world.elements_static[i].material_idx = DEFAULT_MAT_ID;\n        world.elements_static[i].num_connections = 0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic_swap[i].current_energy = 0.0;\n        world.elements_dynamic_swap[i].total_flux = 0.0;\n    }\n\n    // Build connectivity: each element connects to its neighbors in 2D grid\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (int x = 0; x < n_elems_root; ++x) {\n        for (int y = 0; y < n_elems_root; ++y) {\n            const int idx = x * n_elems_root + y;\n            ElementStatic& elem = world.elements_static[idx];\n            \n            // Connect to neighbors (up, down, left, right)\n            const int offsets[4][2] = {{1, 0}, {-1, 0}, {0, 1}, {0, -1}};\n            \n            for (int n = 0; n < 4; ++n) {\n                const int nx = x + offsets[n][0];\n                const int ny = y + offsets[n][1];\n                \n                // Check if neighbor is within bounds\n                if (nx >= 0 && nx < n_elems_root && ny >= 0 && ny < n_elems_root) {\n                    const int neighbor_idx = nx * n_elems_root + ny;\n                    elem.connected_idx[elem.num_connections] = neighbor_idx;\n                    elem.connected_flux[elem.num_connections] = 1.0;\n                    elem.num_connections++;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Set corner elements as inflow/outflow to create interesting dynamics\n    const int last = n_elems_root - 1;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root + 0].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[0 * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = OUTFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + 0].material_idx = OUTFLOW_MAT_ID;\n    world.elements_static[last * n_elems_root + last].material_idx = INFLOW_MAT_ID;\n}\n\n// Compute energy flux between two elements\ninline val_t computeFlux(const Material& mat, const ElementDynamic& this_elem,\n                        val_t connection_flux, const ElementDynamic& other_elem) {\n    return (other_elem.current_energy - this_elem.current_energy) * \n           mat.transfer_coeff * connection_flux * 0.25;\n}\n\n// Run simulation for n_iters iterations\nvoid runSimulation(World& world, const int n_iters) {\n    const size_t n_elems = world.elements_static.size();\n    const ElementStatic* const __restrict__ statics = world.elements_static.data();\n    const Material* const __restrict__ materials = world.materials.data();\n\n    // A single parallel region spans all iterations: threads are forked once and the\n    // implicit barrier of the worksharing loop provides the per-iteration synchronization.\n    // The buffer pointers are thread-private and swapped identically by every thread, so\n    // no additional synchronization is needed for the buffer flip.\n    #pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        ElementDynamic* read_buf = world.elements_dynamic.data();\n        ElementDynamic* write_buf = world.elements_dynamic_swap.data();\n\n        for (int iter = 0; iter < n_iters; ++iter) {\n            // Update all elements\n            #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = 0; i < n_elems; ++i) {\n                const ElementStatic& elem_static = statics[i];\n                const ElementDynamic& elem_dyn = read_buf[i];\n                const Material& mat = materials[elem_static.material_idx];\n\n                // Start with external flow\n                val_t total_flux = mat.external_flow;\n\n                // Add flux from all connected elements\n                for (idx_t j = 0; j < elem_static.num_connections; ++j) {\n                    const idx_t neighbor_idx = elem_static.connected_idx[j];\n                    const ElementDynamic& neighbor_dyn = read_buf[neighbor_idx];\n                    total_flux += computeFlux(mat, elem_dyn, elem_static.connected_flux[j], neighbor_dyn);\n                }\n\n                // Update element state\n                ElementDynamic& elem_write = write_buf[i];\n                elem_write.current_energy = elem_dyn.current_energy + total_flux;\n                elem_write.total_flux = elem_dyn.total_flux + std::abs(total_flux);\n            }\n            // (implicit barrier at the end of the worksharing loop)\n\n            // Swap buffers\n            std::swap(read_buf, write_buf);\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Reflect the buffer flips in the world state: an even number of swaps is a no-op.\n    if (n_iters % 2 != 0) {\n        std::swap(world.elements_dynamic, world.elements_dynamic_swap);\n    }\n}\n\n// Validate simulation results\nbool validateResults(const World& world) {\n    val_t energy_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t flux_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t energy_max = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::lowest();\n    val_t energy_min = std::numeric_limits<val_t>::max();\n    \n    for (const auto& elem : world.elements_dynamic) {\n        energy_sum += elem.current_energy;\n        flux_sum += elem.total_flux;\n        energy_max = std::max(elem.current_energy, energy_max);\n        energy_min = std::min(elem.current_energy, energy_min);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Validation results:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  Energy sum: %.12f\\n\", energy_sum);\n    printf(\"  Flux sum: %.2f\\n\", flux_sum);\n    printf(\"  Energy range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", energy_min, energy_max);\n    \n    // Check for numerical issues\n    constexpr val_t energy_epsilon = 1e-8;\n    \n    if (!std::isfinite(energy_sum)) {\n        printf(\"  ERROR: Energy sum is not finite\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    if (std::abs(energy_sum) > energy_epsilon) {\n        printf(\"  WARNING: Energy sum diverged from 0 (expected conservation)\\n\");\n        // Don't fail validation as this can happen with external flows\n    }\n    \n    if (!std::isfinite(flux_sum)) {\n        printf(\"  ERROR: Flux sum is not finite\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CekF295eRCGTHE1nDiRR9","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done — the benchmark is now unconditionally OpenMP-parallel, bit-exact with the original.\n\n## Changes\n\n**`unstructured/unstructured.cpp`**\n- **Kernel (`runSimulation`)**: one `#pragma omp parallel` region wraps the whole iteration loop, with `#pragma omp for schedule(static)` over the elements. Threads fork once instead of per iteration, and the worksharing loop's implicit barrier supplies the inter-iteration synchronization. The double buffer is flipped via *thread-private* pointers (every thread performs the identical swap, so no sync is needed); the vectors themselves are swapped once at the end if `n_iters` is odd, which is equivalent to swapping them each iteration. Static scheduling gives each thread the same element range every iteration → NUMA/cache locality across iterations.\n- **NUMA first touch**: `std::vector::resize` value-initializes, which would fault in all ~400 MB of pages on the master thread and pin them to one socket. Added a `NoInitAllocator` (default-init instead of value-init) used by the element vectors, so the *first touch* happens in the now-parallel initialization loop, which uses the same iteration space and schedule as the kernel. The connectivity build loop is parallelized too. `sizeof` of the structs is unchanged, so the reported memory usage is identical.\n- **`computeHash`**: parallelized with `reduction(^ : hash)` — XOR is associative/commutative, so it's bit-exact.\n- `validateResults` and the `-r` path were left serial deliberately: they are single O(n) passes outside the timed region, and parallel floating-point reductions would reorder the summation and perturb the printed `Energy sum` / `Sum` digits.\n\n**`unstructured/CMakeLists.txt`**: `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED COMPONENTS CXX)` + link `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`. No new dependencies; executable name unchanged.\n\n## Verification\nCompared against a serial build of the original for `-n {1,100,333,512,1024,2048,4096}` with odd/even/zero iteration counts, at 1–256 threads: `Result hash`, `-r` result block (sum/min/max/samples/hash) and validation output are **byte-identical** in every case.\n\n## Performance (2× EPYC 7763, 128 cores)\n\n| Case | Serial | Parallel (best) | Speedup |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| n=4096, 100 iters | 16.5 s | 1.04 s (64 thr) | ~16× |\n| n=1024, 1000 iters | 9.72 s | 74 ms (256 thr) | ~131× |\n| n=512, 1000 iters | 2.37 s | 22 ms (128 thr) | ~108× |\n\nThe large-grid case saturates at ~280 GB/s of memory traffic (the 144-byte `ElementStatic` record re-read each iteration dominates), i.e. it is memory-bandwidth bound past ~32 threads — that's the ceiling for this data structure, not a synchronization limit. Cache-resident grids scale nearly linearly. I also prototyped non-temporal stores for the write buffer; it gained only ~3% and needs x86 intrinsics plus fencing assumptions around the barrier, so I left it out. Threads fork once instead of per iteration, and the worksharing loop's implicit barrier supplies the inter-iteration synchronization. The double buffer is flipped via *thread-private* pointers (every thread performs the identical swap, so no sync is needed); the vectors themselves are swapped once at the end if `n_iters` is odd, which is equivalent to swapping them each iteration. Static scheduling gives each thread the same element range every iteration → NUMA/cache locality across iterations.\n- **NUMA first touch**: `std::vector::resize` value-initializes, which would fault in all ~400 MB of pages on the master thread and pin them to one socket. Added a `NoInitAllocator` (default-init instead of value-init) used by the element vectors, so the *first touch* happens in the now-parallel initialization loop, which uses the same iteration space and schedule as the kernel. The connectivity build loop is parallelized too. `sizeof` of the structs is unchanged, so the reported memory usage is identical.\n- **`computeHash`**: parallelized with `reduction(^ : hash)` — XOR is associative/commutative, so it's bit-exact.\n- `validateResults` and the `-r` path were left serial deliberately: they are single O(n) passes outside the timed region, and parallel floating-point reductions would reorder the summation and perturb the printed `Energy sum` / `Sum` digits.\n\n**`unstructured/CMakeLists.txt`**: `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED COMPONENTS CXX)` + link `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`. No new dependencies; executable name unchanged.\n\n## Verification\nCompared against a serial build of the original for `-n {1,100,333,512,1024,2048,4096}` with odd/even/zero iteration counts, at 1–256 threads: `Result hash`, `-r` result block (sum/min/max/samples/hash) and validation output are **byte-identical** in every case.\n\n## Performance (2× EPYC 7763, 128 cores)\n\n| Case | Serial | Parallel (best) | Speedup |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| n=4096, 100 iters | 16.5 s | 1.04 s (64 thr) | ~16× |\n| n=1024, 1000 iters | 9.72 s | 74 ms (256 thr) | ~131× |\n| n=512, 1000 iters | 2.37 s | 22 ms (128 thr) | ~108× |\n\nThe large-grid case saturates at ~280 GB/s of memory traffic (the 144-byte `ElementStatic` record re-read each iteration dominates), i.e. it is memory-bandwidth bound past ~32 threads — that's the ceiling for this data structure, not a synchronization limit. Cache-resident grids scale nearly linearly. I also prototyped non-temporal stores for the write buffer; it gained only ~3% and needs x86 intrinsics plus fencing assumptions around the barrier, so I left it out.
